\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt)
with an assignment

\sigma:\Prop\to A
. Analogous to Section
0.3.1
,
the interpretation of proposition letters is given by the assignment,
and the connectives and modalities as interpreted via their counterparts
in
{A}
.
This gives rise to validity of formulae and modal consequence pairs in
a modal lattice
{A}
.

If
\mathfrak{M}=(\mathfrak{X},V)
is a modal L-model then
V
is an assignment
for
\mathfrak{X}^{*}
and we write
\mathfrak{M}^{*}=(\mathfrak{X}^{*},V)
.
If
\mathfrak{M}=(\mathfrak{X},V)
is a principal modal L-model then

V
is an assignment for
\mathfrak{X}^{\ddagger}
and we let

\mathfrak{M}^{\ddagger}=(\mathfrak{X}^{\ddagger},V)
.
We obtain the following counterpart of Lemma
0.3.9
.

0.4.15 Lemma
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}
be a modal L-model,
\mathfrak{N}
a principal modal L-model,
and
\varphi\in\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.98pt{\vbox to3.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.98917pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
.
Then

\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{M}}=\llparenthesis\kern 0.86108pt\varphi\kern 0.86108pt\rrparenthesis_{\mathfrak{M}^{*}}\quad\text{and}\quad\llbracket\varphi\rrbracket^{\mathfrak{N}}=\llparenthesis\kern 0.86108pt\varphi\kern 0.86108pt\rrparenthesis_{\mathfrak{N}^{\ddagger}}.

We write
\varphi\mathrel{\kern 0.43057pt\text{\hbox to7.06pt{\vbox to7.53pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.10971pt\lower-3.76735pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.92 L 0 4.92}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 6.55 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}{}{}{}}}{}
{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.62 0 C -2.62 -1.95 -1.67 -3.77 -0.08 -4.89}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}{}{}{}}}{}
{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.62 0 C -2.62 1.95 -1.67 3.77 -0.08 4.89}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}${}_{\Gamma}$}}\kern 0.43057pt\psi
if any modal lattice that
validates all consequence pairs in
\Gamma
also validates

\varphi\trianglelefteqslant\psi
.
Then we can prove the next theorem in the same way as in
Section
0.3.1
.

0.4.15 Theorem
.

Let
\Gamma\cup\{\varphi\trianglelefteqslant\psi\}
be a set of modal consequence pairs.
Then
\varphi\vdash_{\Gamma}\psi
iff
\varphi\mathrel{\kern 0.43057pt\text{\hbox to7.06pt{\vbox to7.53pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.10971pt\lower-3.76735pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.92 L 0 4.92}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 6.55 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}{}{}{}}}{}
{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.62 0 C -2.62 -1.95 -1.67 -3.77 -0.08 -4.89}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}{}{}{}}}{}
{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.62 0 C -2.62 1.95 -1.67 3.77 -0.08 4.89}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}${}_{\Gamma}$}}\kern 0.43057pt\psi
.

0.4.3
Modal L-spaces and Duality

We define the modal counterpart of L-spaces as follows.

0.4.16 Definition
.

A
modal L-space
is a tuple
(X,1,\curlywedge,\tau,R)
such that

1.

(X,1,\curlywedge,\tau)
is an L-space;

2.

R
is a binary relation on
X
such that

1Rx
iff
x=1
for all
x\in X
;

3.

If
a
is a clopen filter, then so are
[R]a
and
\langle R\rangle a
;

4.

For all
x,y\in X
we have
xRy
if and only if for all
a\in\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}
:

•

If
x\in[R]a
then
y\in a
;

•

If
y\in a
then
x\in\langle R\rangle a
.

Truth and validity in modal L-spaces is defined as usual, using
clopen valuations.

The third item is a condition often seen in the definition of general
frames. Item (
4
) is our counterpart of the tightness
condition, and has previously been used in
[
7
, Section 2]
.
Next, we prove that each modal L-space has an underlying
(principal) modal L-frame.

0.4.17 Lemma
.

Let
\mathbb{X}=(X,1,\curlywedge,\tau,R)
be a modal L-space.
Then
R[x]
is closed for all
x\in X
.

0.4.18 Proof
.

Suppose
y\notin R[x]
.
Then there exists a clopen filter
a
such that either
x\in[R]a

and
y\notin a
, or
y\in a
and
x\notin\langle R\rangle a
.
In the first case
X\setminus a
is a clopen neighbourhood
of
y
disjoint from
R[x]
. In the second case

a
is a clopen neighbourhood of
y
disjoint from
R[x]
.

0.4.19 Proposition
.

Let
\mathbb{X}=(X,1,\curlywedge,\tau,R)
be a modal L-space.
Then
(X,1,\curlywedge,R)
is a principal modal L-frame.

0.4.20 Proof
.

We know that L-spaces have all non-empty meets, and hence also binary
joins, so (
0
) is satisfied.
Furthermore, (
5
) is satisfied by definition.
We verify the other conditions from Definition
0.4.1
,
starting with (

4
′

).

Condition

4
′

. Suppose
x_{i}Ry_{i}
, where
i
ranges over some index set
I
.
If
I=\emptyset
then this condition states
1R1
which holds by definition,
so assume that
I\neq\emptyset
.
By the tightness condition of modal L-spaces, in order to prove

(\bigcurlywedge x_{i})R(\bigcurlywedge y_{i})
it suffices to show that for all
clopen filters
a
,
\bigcurlywedge x_{i}\in[R]a
implies
\bigcurlywedge y_{i}\in a

and
\bigcurlywedge y_{i}\in a
implies
\bigcurlywedge x_{i}\in\langle R\rangle a
.

First assume
\bigcurlywedge x_{i}\in[R]a
. Since
[R]a
is a clopen filter
and
\bigcurlywedge x_{i}\preccurlyeq x_{j}
for each
j\in I
we have

x_{j}\in[R]a
, so
R[x_{j}]\subseteq a
. By assumption
x_{j}Ry_{j}
,
so
y_{j}\in a
for all
j\in I
. Since
a
is a clopen filter it is
principal, hence
\bigcurlywedge y_{i}\in a
.
Second, suppose
\bigcurlywedge y_{i}\in a
.
Then
y_{j}\in a
for all
j\in I
,
which implies
x_{j}\in\langle R\rangle a
for all
j\in I
.
Since
\langle R\rangle a
is a clopen filter, hence principal,
we find
\bigcurlywedge x_{i}\in\langle R\rangle a
.

Condition
1
. Let
x\preccurlyeq y
and
yRz
.
Suppose towards a contradiction that there exists no
w\in X

such that
xRw
and
w\preccurlyeq z
.
Let
x^{\prime}=\bigcurlywedge R[x]
be the minimal element in
R[x]

(which is an
R
-successor of
x
by (

4
′

)).
Then
x^{\prime}\not\preccurlyeq z
, so we can find a clopen filter
a
containing

x^{\prime}
such that
z\notin a
. This implies
R[x]\subseteq a
,
so that
x\in[R]a
, but
y\notin[R]a
because

yRz
and
z\notin a
. As
x\preccurlyeq y
this violates the
fact that
[R]a
is a filter.

Condition
2
. Let
x\preccurlyeq y
and
xRw
. Suppose towards a contradiction that
there exists no
z\in X
such that
yRz
and
w\preccurlyeq z
.
Then
R[y]\cap{\uparrow}w=\emptyset
. Both
R[y]
and

{\uparrow}z
are closed, as a consequence of Lemmas
0.4.17

and
0.2.9
and the fact that singletons in
a Stone spaces are always closed.
Therefore we can find a clopen filter
a
containing
{\uparrow}z

which is disjoint from
R[y]
. This implies that

x\in\langle R\rangle a
while
y\notin\langle R\rangle a
.
Since
x\preccurlyeq y
this contradicts the fact that
\langle R\rangle a

is a filter.

Condition

3
′

. Finally, let
\{x_{i}\mid i\in I\}
be some collection of
elements of
X
and suppose
(\bigcurlywedge x_{i})Rz
.
If
I
is empty then
\bigcurlywedge x_{i}=1
and
Definition
0.4.16
(
2
) implies
z=1
,
and the empty collection witnesses truth of (

3
′

).
So assume
I\neq\emptyset
.
Since
\bigcurlywedge x_{i}\preccurlyeq x_{j}
for all
j\in I
,
condition (
2
) implies that
R[x_{j}]\neq\emptyset
for all

j\in I
. As a consequence of (

4
′

) there is a smallest
element
z_{j}:=\bigcurlywedge R[x_{j}]
in each
R[x_{j}]
.
We claim that
\bigcurlywedge z_{j}\leq z
.
Suppose not, then there is a clopen filter
a
containing
\bigcurlywedge z_{j}

such that
z\notin a
. This implies
x_{j}\in[R]a
for all
j\in I
,
but
\bigcurlywedge x_{i}\notin[R]a
because
(\bigcurlywedge x_{i})Rz
and
z\notin a
.
But this contradicts the fact that
[R]a
is principal filter.

This proposition motivates the following definition.

0.4.21 Definition
.

Let
\mathbb{X}=(X,1,\curlywedge,\tau,R)
be a modal L-space.
Then we write
\pi\mathbb{X}=(X,1,\curlywedge,R)
for the underlying
principal modal L-frame, and
\kappa\mathbb{X}=(X,1,\curlywedge,R)
for the
underlying principal modal L-frame regarded as a modal L-frame.

While they may appear the same, the difference between
\pi\mathbb{X}
and

\kappa\mathbb{X}
lies in the valuations they allow for. While valuations of

\pi\mathbb{X}
necessarily interpret proposition letters as principal filters,
a valuation for
\kappa\mathbb{X}
can assign any filter to a proposition letter.
As a consequence, both frames differ in terms of
validity
.

For future reference, we prove the following lemma about
modal L-spaces. It states that the action of
[R]
and
\langle R\rangle

on any filter is determined by their action on clopen filters.

0.4.22 Lemma
.

Let
\mathbb{X}=(X,1,\curlywedge,\tau,R)
be a modal L-space.
The for every closed filter
c\in\mathcal{F}_{k}(\mathbb{X})
,

[R]c=\bigcap\big\{[R]a\mid a\in\mathcal{F}_{clp}(\mathbb{X}),c\subseteq a\big\}\quad\text{and}\quad\langle R\rangle c=\bigcap\big\{\langle R\rangle a\mid a\in\mathcal{F}_{clp}(\mathbb{X}),c\subseteq a\big\}.

(9)

Furthermore, for every filter
d\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbb{X})
we have

[R]d=\operatorname{\bigtriangledown}\big\{[R]c\mid c\in\mathcal{F}_{k}(\mathbb{X}),c\subseteq d\big\}\quad\text{and}\quad\langle R\rangle d=\operatorname{\bigtriangledown}\big\{\langle R\rangle c\mid c\in\mathcal{F}_{k}(\mathbb{X}),c\subseteq d\big\}.

(10)

0.4.23 Proof
.

The left-to-right inclusion of the first equality follows from the fact
that
c\subseteq a
implies
[R]c\subseteq[R]a
.
For the converse, suppose
x\in\mathbb{X}
is such that

x\notin[R]c
. Then
R[x]\not\subseteq c
, so there is some

y\in R[x]
such that
y\notin c
.
Since
c
is a closed filter, it is the intersection of all clopen filters
that contain it. A routine compactness argument yields a clopen
filter
b
containing
c
such that
y\notin b
.
Therefore
R[x]\not\subseteq b
, so
x\notin[R]b

and hence
x
is not in the right hand side of the equality.

For the second equality, it suffices to prove the right-to-left inclusion as well.
Suppose
x\notin\langle R\rangle c
. Then
R[x]\cap c=\emptyset
.
Since
R[x]
is closed, we can use compactness to find some clopen filter
a

containing
c
disjoint from
R[x]
, and the argument proceeds as above.

Next, consider (
10
).
We know that
c\subseteq d
implies
[R]c\subseteq[R]d
,
and since the latter is a filter containing
[R]c

for all closed filters
c\subseteq d
, it contains the filter generated
by them. This proves
\supseteq
.
Conversely, suppose
x\in[R]d
.
Then
R[x]\subseteq d
.
Since
d
is up-closed we also have
{\uparrow}R[x]\subseteq d
.
The set
{\uparrow}R[x]
is closed by Lemmas
0.4.17

and
0.2.9
,
and it is a filter because
R[x]
is closed under
\curlywedge
.
Thus we have found a closed filter
c:={\uparrow}R[x]
contained in
d
such that

x\in[R]c
. This proves
\subseteq
.

The right-to-left inclusion of the second equality in (
10
)
is easy again. For
\subseteq
,
suppose
x\in\langle R\rangle d
. Then
R[x]\cap d\neq\emptyset
,
so we can find some
y\in R[x]\cap d
.
Now
{\uparrow}y
is a closed filter contained in
d

such that
x\in\langle R\rangle({\uparrow}y)
, witnessing
\subseteq
.

Since we know that each modal L-space has an underlying modal L-frame,
we can now conveniently define the morphisms between them as follows.

0.4.24 Definition
.

A
modal L-space morphism
from
(X,1,\curlywedge,\tau,R)
to

(X^{\prime},1^{\prime},\curlywedge^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},R^{\prime})
is a function
f:X\to X^{\prime}
such that

f:(X,1,\curlywedge,\tau)\to(X^{\prime},1^{\prime},\curlywedge^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
is an L-space morphism
and
f:(X,1,\curlywedge,R)\to(X^{\prime},1^{\prime},\curlywedge^{\prime},R^{\prime})
is a bounded L-morphism.
We denote the resulting category by
\mathsf{MLSpace}
.

We work towards a duality between modal lattices and modal L-spaces.

0.4.25 Proposition
.

If
\mathbb{X}=(X,1,\curlywedge,\tau,R)
is a modal L-space
then
(\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X},[R],\langle R\rangle)
is a modal lattice.
Moreover, if
f:\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
is a modal L-space morphism,
then

\mathcal{F}_{clp}f=f^{-1}:(\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}^{\prime},[R^{\prime}],\langle R^{\prime}\rangle)\to(\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X},[R],\langle R\rangle)

is a modal lattice homomorphism.

0.4.26 Proof
.

The maps
[R],\langle R\rangle
are functions on
\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}

by definition.
It follows from Proposition
0.4.19
and
Lemma
0.4.9
that they satisfy the conditions from
Definition
0.4.13
.

If
f
is a modal L-space morphism then in particular it is an
L-space morphism, so
\mathcal{F}_{clp}f
is a lattice homomorphism
from
\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to
\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}
.
So we only have to show that
f^{-1}
preserves the modalities.
This can be proven in the same way as in
Proposition
0.4.7
.

We now show how to turn a modal lattice into a modal L-space.

0.4.27 Definition
.

Let
{A}=(A,\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt,\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

we need to prove

\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

For the right one, suppose

.

.

we have
pR_{A}\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}

and
by assumption
a\in\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

. This implies that

and therefore
p\in\theta_{A}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta)
.
For the reverse inclusion, suppose
p\in\theta_{A}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta)
.
Then
a\in\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{-1}(p)
, so
pR_{A}q
implies
a\in q
,
and hence
p\in[R_{A}]\theta_{A}(a)
.

Next, suppose
p\in\langle R_{A}\rangle\theta_{A}(a)
.
Then there exists a filter
q
such that
pR_{A}q
and
a\in q
.
By definition of
R_{A}
this implies
a\in\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{-1}(p)
and hence

\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

, so
p\in\theta_{A}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

.
Conversely, suppose
p\in\theta_{A}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta)
.
Let
q
be the filter generated by
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{-1}(p)
and

.

implies
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}

.
To see this, note that for each

there exists some

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{-1}(p)

such that

.
We then have
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}

, and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta\in p
by assumption,

\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pta\in p
.

.

0.4.33 Proof
.

We verify the condition from Definition
0.4.16
.
Item (
1
) follows from Theorem
0.2.12
.
Item (
2
) follows from Lemma
0.4.28
.
Item (
3
) follows from Lemma
0.4.30
.
Item (
4
) follows from the definition
of
R_{A}
and the fact that each clopen filter is of the form

\theta_{A}(a)
.

0.4.34 Lemma
.

Let
h:{A}\to{A}^{\prime}
be a modal lattice homomorphism.
Then
\mathcal{F}h=h^{-1}:{A}^{\prime}_{*}\to{A}_{*}
is a bounded
L-space morphism.

0.4.35 Proof
.

It follows from the duality between lattices and L-spaces that

h^{-1}
is an L-space morphism, so we only have to verify the
three conditions from Definition
0.4.6
.
We write
R^{\prime}
and
R
for the relations from
{A}^{\prime}_{*}
and

{A}_{*}
.

1.

and

be filters of

(elements of

)
such that

. In order to prove that

}

this implies

, as desired.

3.

Suppose
h^{-1}(p^{\prime})Rq
. Then
{\uparrow}h[q]
is a filter
(since
h
is a lattice homomorphism it is non-empty and closed under meets).
Let
q^{\prime}
be the filter generated by
{\uparrow}h[q]
and

(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\prime})^{-1}(p^{\prime})
. Then
q\subseteq h^{-1}(q^{\prime})
by construction,
so it suffices to show that
p^{\prime}R^{\prime}q^{\prime}
.
We have
(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}

so we only have to show that
q^{\prime}\subseteq(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

By construction we have
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\prime}b^{\prime}\in p^{\prime}
.
Furthermore,
c\in q
implies
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}

.
Therefore

, and consequently

p^{\prime}R^{\prime}q^{\prime}
.

Using the above lemmas we now establish a duality for modal lattices.

0.4.35 Theorem
.

The duality between
\mathsf{Lat}
and
\mathsf{LSpace}
lifts to a duality

\mathsf{MLat}\equiv^{\op}\mathsf{MLSpace}.

0.4.36 Proof
.

Let
\mathbb{X}=(X,\leq,\tau,R)
be a modal L-space.
This gives rise to the modal lattice

\mathbb{X}^{*}=(\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X},[R],\langle R\rangle)
,
which in turn yields a modal L-space

(\mathbb{X}^{*})_{*}=(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X},\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X},\cap,\tau_{\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}},R_{\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}})
.
As a consequence of the duality for lattices (Theorem
0.2.12
)
we know that
(X,1,\curlywedge,\tau)
is isomorphic to
(\mathcal{F}_{b}\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X},A,\cap,\tau_{\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}})

via
x\mapsto\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(x)=\{a\in\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}\mid x\in a\}
.
So we only have to show that
R
and
R_{\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}}
coincide.
This can be seen as follows:

\displaystyle xRy

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\forall a\in\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}\big(x\in[R]a\text{ implies }y\in a\text{ and }y\in a\text{ implies }\langle R\rangle a\big)

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\forall a\in\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}\big([R]a\in\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(x)\text{ implies }a\in\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(y)\text{ and }a\in\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(y)\text{ implies }\langle R\rangle a\in\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(x)\big)

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\forall a\in\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}\big([R]^{-1}(\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(x))\subseteq\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(y)\subseteq\langle R\rangle^{-1}(\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(x))\big)

\displaystyle\quad\text{iff}\quad\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(x)R_{\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathbb{X}}\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(y)

Next, let

be a modal lattice,

{A}_{*}=(\mathcal{F}A,R_{A})
and

({A}_{*})^{*}=(\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathcal{F}A,[R_{A}],\langle R_{A}\rangle)
.
Then the duality for lattices from Theorem
0.2.12

tells us that
A
and
\mathcal{F}_{clp}\mathcal{F}A
are isomorphic via

a\mapsto\theta_{A}(a)=\{p\in\mathcal{F}A\mid a\in p\}
,
so we just have to show that
coincides with
[R_{A}]
and

coincides with
\langle R_{A}\rangle
.
That is, we have to show that
\theta_{A}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}

and

\theta_{A}(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

.
But we have already proven that in Lemma
0.4.30
.

The two paragraphs above establish the duality on objects.
The duality for morphisms follows immediately from Theorem
0.2.12

and the fact that all our categories are concrete.

(11)

so both the collection of closed filters as well as the collection of
all filters form modal lattices.

and

0.4.39 Proof
.

Let
\mathbb{X}=(X,1,\curlywedge,\tau,R_{A})
be the modal L-space dual
to
{A}
. By Proposition
0.2.7
we can identify
the filter extension of
A
with the lattice of closed filters of

\mathbb{X}
with inclusion
\theta:A\to\mathcal{F}_{k}(\mathbb{X})
.
In
\mathcal{F}_{k}(\mathbb{X}
, the top, bottom and meet are given by
X
,
\{1\}

and intersection, and the join of a collection of filters is the smallest
filter containing their union.
So we only have to verify that for all
c\in\mathcal{F}_{k}(\mathbb{X})
we
have
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\FiltComp}c=[R_{A}]c
and
\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt^{\FiltComp}c=\langle R_{A}\rangle c
.
Thus compute

\displaystyle\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\big\{\theta(\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

0.4.40 Corollary
.

The filter and
\Pi_{1}
-completion of a modal lattice are
modal lattices themselves.

0.4.41

We use following definition:
if

and

{A}
,
then we define the valuation
\theta_{1}\wedge\theta_{2}
by

(\theta_{1}\wedge\theta_{2})(p):=\theta_{1}(p)\wedge\theta_{2}(p)
.

0.4.41 Lemma
.

Let
{A}=(A,\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt,\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt)
be a modal lattice,

\FiltComp({A})
its filter completion,
and
\sigma
a valuation of the proposition letters for
\FiltComp({A})
.

1.

If
a\in A
and
\varphi\in\mathbf{L_{\hskip 0.62221pt\text{\hbox to3.98pt{\vbox to3.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.98917pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.62221pt\!\hskip 0.62221pt\text{\hbox to4.62pt{\vbox to4.62pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.31197pt\lower-2.31197pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.62221pt}}
are such
that
a\in\sigma(\varphi)
, then there exists a valuation

\theta
for
{A}
such that
\theta(p)\in\sigma(p)
for
all
p\in\Prop
and
\theta(\varphi)\leq a
.

2.

If
\theta
is a valuation for
{A}
such that

\theta(p)\in\sigma(p)
for all
p
then
\theta(\varphi)\in\sigma(\varphi)
for all
\varphi\in\mathbf{L_{\hskip 0.62221pt\text{\hbox to3.98pt{\vbox to3.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.98917pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.62221pt\!\hskip 0.62221pt\text{\hbox to4.62pt{\vbox to4.62pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.31197pt\lower-2.31197pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -2.95 M -0.03 -2.92 L -2.92 -0.03 C -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 0.01 -2.92 0.03 L -0.03 2.92 C -0.01 2.94 0.01 2.94 0.03 2.92 L 2.92 0.03 C 2.94 0.01 2.94 -0.01 2.92 -0.03 L 0.03 -2.92 C 0.01 -2.94 -0.01 -2.94 -0.03 -2.92 Z M 0 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.62221pt}}
.

0.4.42 Proof
.

We start with the first statement. We proceed by induction on the
structure of
\varphi
.
If
\varphi=p\in\Prop
then we set
\theta(p)=a
and
\theta(q)=\top

for all other
q\in\Prop
.
If
\varphi=\top
then
\theta(\top)=\top\in\{\top\}=\sigma(\top)
,
and if
\varphi=\bot
then
\theta(\bot)=\bot\in A=\sigma(\bot)
.

Suppose
\varphi=\varphi_{1}\wedge\varphi_{2}
.
If
a\in\sigma(\varphi_{1}\wedge\varphi_{2})=\sigma(\varphi_{1})\mathbin{\triangledown}\sigma(\varphi_{2})

then there are
a_{1}\in\sigma(\varphi_{1})
and
a_{2}\in\sigma(\varphi_{2})

such that
a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\leq a
.
The induction hypothesis then gives valuations
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
for

{A}
such that
\theta_{1}(\varphi_{1})\leq a_{1}
and
\theta_{2}(\varphi_{2})\leq a_{2}
,
and such that
\theta_{1}(p)\in\sigma_{1}(p)
and
\theta_{2}(p)\in\sigma_{2}(p)

for all
p\in\Prop
.
Let
\theta=\theta_{1}\wedge\theta_{2}
.
Since
\theta_{i}(p)\in\sigma(p)
for all
p\in\Prop
, and
\sigma(p)

is a filter, we have
\theta(p)\in\sigma(p)
for all
p\in\Prop
.
Then
\theta(\varphi_{i})\leq\theta_{i}(\varphi_{i})
for
i\in\{1,2\}
,
and we find

\theta(\varphi_{1}\wedge\varphi_{2})=\theta(\varphi_{1})\wedge\theta(\varphi_{2})\leq\theta_{1}(\varphi_{1})\wedge\theta_{2}(\varphi_{2})\leq a_{1}\wedge a_{2}\leq a.

If
\varphi=\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}
, then

a\in\sigma(\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2})=\sigma(\varphi_{1})\cap\sigma(\varphi_{2})

so there are valuations
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
such that

\theta_{1}(\varphi_{1})\leq a
and
\theta_{2}(\varphi_{2})\leq a
,
and such that
\theta_{1}(p)\in\sigma_{1}(p)
and
\theta_{2}(p)\in\sigma_{2}(p)

for all
p\in\Prop
.
Let again
\theta=\theta_{1}\wedge\theta_{2}
. Then just as above

\theta(p)\in\sigma(p)
for all
p\in\Prop
. Moreover,
\theta(\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2})=\theta(\varphi_{1})\vee\theta(\varphi_{2})\leq\theta_{1}(\varphi_{1})\vee\theta_{2}(\varphi_{2})\leq a\vee a=a.

Finally, let
\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\in\{\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt,\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\}

and
\varphi=\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\varphi_{1}
.
Suppose
a\in\sigma(\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\varphi_{1})
. Then

a\in\sigma(\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\varphi_{1})=\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}^{\FiltComp}\sigma(\varphi_{1})=\bigwedge\{{\uparrow}(\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}b)\mid b\in A,\sigma(\varphi_{1})\leq{\uparrow}b\}=\bigwedge\{{\uparrow}(\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}b)\mid b\in A,b\in\sigma(\varphi_{1})\}.

The last equality follows from the fact that filters are ordered by reverse
inclusion, so that
\sigma(\varphi_{1})\leq{\uparrow}b
iff

\sigma(\varphi_{1})\supseteq{\uparrow}b
iff
b\in\sigma(\varphi_{1})
.
Since
\bigwedge
in
\FiltComp({A})
is defined as
\operatorname{\bigtriangledown}
,
there exist
b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}\in A
such that
b_{i}\in\sigma(\varphi_{1})

for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and

\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}b_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}b_{n}\leq a
.
The induction hypothesis implies that there exist valuations
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{n}

for
{A}
such that
\theta_{i}(\varphi_{1})\leq b_{i}

and
\theta_{i}(p)\in\sigma(p)
for all
p\in\Prop
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.
Since

is monotone we find

\theta_{i}(\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\varphi_{1})=\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\theta_{i}(\varphi_{1})\leq\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}b_{i}
for all
i
.
Letting
\theta=\theta_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\theta_{n}
gives

\theta(\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\varphi_{1})\leq\theta_{i}(\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\varphi_{1})\leq\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}b_{i}
for all
i
,
so that
\theta(\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\varphi_{1})\leq\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}b_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}b_{n}\leq a
,
as desired.
Since
\sigma(p)
is a filter and
\theta_{i}(p)\in\sigma(p)
for all

i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
we find
\theta(p)\in\sigma(p)
for all
p\in\Prop
.

We prove the second item by induction on the structure of
\varphi
as well.
The base cases
\varphi=\top,\bot,p\in\Prop
are routine.
Suppose
\varphi=\varphi_{1}\wedge\varphi_{2}
. By induction we have

\theta(\varphi_{1})\in\sigma(\varphi_{1})
and
\theta(\varphi_{2})\in\sigma(\varphi_{2})
.
So

\theta(\varphi_{1}\wedge\varphi_{2})=\theta(\varphi_{1})\wedge\theta(\varphi_{2})\in\sigma(\varphi_{1})\mathbin{\triangledown}\sigma(\varphi_{2})=\sigma(\varphi_{1}\wedge\varphi_{2}).

If
\varphi=\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2}
, then with the same induction hypothesis
we find
\theta(\varphi_{i})\leq\theta(\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2})
so

\theta(\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2})\in\sigma(\varphi_{i})
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Therefore

\theta(\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2})\in\sigma(\varphi_{1})\cap\sigma(\varphi_{2})=\sigma(\varphi_{1}\vee\varphi_{2})
.
Lastly suppose
\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\in\{\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to5.68pt{\vbox to5.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.84166pt\lower-2.84166pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.57 -3.57 M -3.57 -3.51 L -3.57 3.51 C -3.57 3.55 -3.55 3.57 -3.51 3.57 L 3.51 3.57 C 3.55 3.57 3.57 3.55 3.57 3.51 L 3.57 -3.51 C 3.57 -3.55 3.55 -3.57 3.51 -3.57 L -3.51 -3.57 C -3.55 -3.57 -3.57 -3.55 -3.57 -3.51 Z M 3.57 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt,\hskip 0.86108pt\text{\hbox to6.61pt{\vbox to6.61pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\;\lower-3.3028pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -4.21 M -0.04 -4.17 L -4.17 -0.04 C -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 0.02 -4.17 0.04 L -0.04 4.17 C -0.02 4.19 0.02 4.19 0.04 4.17 L 4.17 0.04 C 4.19 0.02 4.19 -0.02 4.17 -0.04 L 0.04 -4.17 C 0.02 -4.19 -0.02 -4.19 -0.04 -4.17 Z M 0 4.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.86108pt\}
and
\varphi=\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\varphi_{1}
.
By the induction hypothesis we have
\theta(\varphi_{1})\in\sigma(\varphi_{1})
,
so
\sigma(\varphi_{1})\supseteq{\uparrow}\theta(\varphi_{1})
and since

\FiltComp({A})
is ordered by reverse inclusion, it follows from
the definition of
\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}^{\FiltComp}
that

\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}^{\FiltComp}\sigma(\varphi_{1})\supseteq{\uparrow}\scalebox{.75}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faStarO}}}\theta(\varphi_{1})
,
hence

We can now prove the theorem.

.

We note that the proof of Lemma
0.4.41
only relies on monotonicity
of the modalities. Thus it yields an analogue of Theorem
0.4.3

for lattices with monotone operators.

The arguments from Lemma
0.4.41
and
Theorem
0.4.3
can be dualised to similar results for
ideal completions.
The ideal completion of a modal lattice need not give rise
to a modal lattice, but it does give rise to a lattice with two monotone
operators, and the modal cases from Lemma
0.4.41
and
Theorem
0.4.3
only rely on monotonicity of the modalities.
Thus we obtain a similar results for the ideal completion and hence for the

\Pi_{1}
-completion of a modal lattice.

We can use this algebraic result to obtain completeness
for weak positive modal logics.
Theorem

yields the following analogue
of Lemma

0.4.45 Proof
.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
0.3.3
.

0.4.5
Sahlqvist Correspondence

We extend the results from Section
0.3.4
to obtain
Sahlqvist correspondence for modal L-frames. Our definition
of a Sahlqvist consequence pair is closely aligned to Sahlqivst formulae
from normal modal logic (see e.g.
[
6
, Definition 3.51]
).
To account for the additional relation in the definition of a modal L-model,
we work with a first-order logic with an extra binary relation symbol
(compared to Section
0.3.4
).
That is, we let
\mathbf{FOL}_{2}
be the first-order logic with a unary predicate
for each proposition letter and two binary predicates

S
(corresponding to the partial order) and
R
(corresponding to the modal relation).
We write
\mathbf{SOL}_{2}
for the second-order logic with the same predicates
where we allow quantification over unary predicates.
Every modal L-model
\mathfrak{M}
gives rise to a first-order structure

\mathfrak{M}^{\circ}
for
\mathbf{FOL}_{2}
in the obvious way,
and similarly every modal L-frame
\mathfrak{X}
yields a structure

\mathfrak{X}^{\circ}
where we can interpret
\mathbf{SOL}_{2}
-formulae with no free predicates.
We extend the standard translation from Definition
0.3.29
to a
translation
\st_{x}:\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.98pt{\vbox to3.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.98917pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.62pt{\vbox to4.62pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.31197pt\lower-2.31197pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

-formula. Then

Defining the second-order translation of a modal consequence pair

\varphi\trianglelefteqslant\psi
as in Definition
0.3.32
,
we can extend Lemma
0.3.33
to the next lemma:

0.4.47 Lemma
.

For all modal L-frames
\mathfrak{X}=(X,\leq,R)
and modal
consequence pairs
\psi\trianglelefteqslant\chi
,

\mathfrak{X}\Vdash\psi\trianglelefteqslant\chi\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathfrak{X}^{\circ}\models\so(\psi\trianglelefteqslant\chi).

Finally, we still have monotonicity of all connectives of

\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.98pt{\vbox to3.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.98917pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.5 -2.5 M -2.5 -2.46 L -2.5 2.46 C -2.5 2.48 -2.48 2.5 -2.46 2.5 L 2.46 2.5 C 2.48 2.5 2.5 2.48 2.5 2.46 L 2.5 -2.46 C 2.5 -2.48 2.48 -2.5 2.46 -2.5 L -2.46 -2.5 C -2.48 -2.5 -2.5 -2.48 -2.5 -2.46 Z M 2.5 2.5}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

, so the following analogue of

goes through without problems.

0.4.48 Lemma
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a modal L-frame and let
V
and
V^{\prime}
be valuations for
\mathfrak{X}

such that
V(p)\subseteq V^{\prime}(p)
for all
p\in\Prop
.
Then for all
\varphi\in\mathbf{L}_{\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to3.98pt{\vbox to3.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-1.98917pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt\!\hskip 0.60275pt\text{\hbox to4.62pt{\vbox to4.62pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.31197pt\lower-2.31197pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\kern 0.60275pt}
we have
V(\varphi)\subseteq V^{\prime}(\varphi)
.

We are now ready to define Sahlqvist consequence pairs and prove
a correspondence result. We make use of the following notion of
a boxed atom.

0.4.49 Definition
.

A
boxed atom
is a formula of the form

}
